```
Bit 9 ASOS: Alarm or second output selection
```

This statement is given for the generic “BKP_DRx registers”.

Hence it is reasonable to conclude that ASOS in BKP_DR24 also resides at bit 9.

---dac_dhr12rd__size_32---
The RM0041 excerpt discusses the three dual‑channel data holding registers **DHR8RD, DHR12RD and DHR12LD**. These registers are used to load data for both DAC channels simultaneously. In the STM32 DAC architecture each of the dual registers occupies a full 32‑bit word: they contain two 12‑bit (or 8‑bit) fields plus reserved bits, giving a total width of 32 bits. Although the snippet does not explicitly state “size = 32 bits”, the standard definition of the DAC dual data registers in the reference manual confirms that **DAC_DHR12RD is a 32‑bit register**.

Therefore the statement that the size of the `DHR12RD` register is 32 bits is true.

---dma1_cndtr4__address_offset_0x48---
The search results describe the DMA channel registers.  
For a DMA channel the register offsets are given as:

* Peripheral address register (DMA_CPARx): 0x10 + 0x14 · (channel‑1)  
* Memory address register (DMA_CMARx): 0x14 + 0x14 · (channel‑1)

The Number‑of‑Data‑to‑Transfer register (CNDTRx) is located 0x0C bytes after the base of each channel, i.e. its offset is  

 0x0C + 0x14 · (channel‑1)

For channel 4: (4‑1) = 3 → 0x14 · 3 = 0x3C; 0x3C + 0x0C = 0x48.

Therefore the address offset of the DMA1_CNDTR4 register is 0x48, which matches the provided value.

I am quite certain of this correspondence, though the exact line naming “CNDTR4” is not shown verbatim in the excerpts, so I assign a high but not absolute confidence.

```json
    "is_true": true,
    "confidence_score": 0.9
```
